REPORT OF THE TREASURER 

1952 1953 

TRUSTEES OF TUFTS COLLEGE MEDFORD, MASSACHUSETTS DECEMBER 1953

NATHAN D. BUGBEE TREASURER 50 CONGRESS STREET BOSTON MASS. 

TRUSTEES OF TUFTS COLLEGE BALLOU HALL HARVEY E. AVERILL ASSISTANT TREASURER SECRETARY MEDFORD 55, MASSACHUSETTS 

December 2, 1953 

To the Trustees of Tufts College 

You will find in the following pages the financial report of the College for the year ended June 30, 1953, accompanied by the certificate of the auditors, Scovell, Wellington & Company.

Income and Expenses 

The following table shows the income and expenditures of the College during the past two years:

Surplus 

The balance sheet surplus on June 30, 1953, was $124,283.13 as compared with $108,078.00 at the end of the previous year, an increase of $16,205.13. 

Plant Account 

The total plant account as of June 30, 1953, was $6,916,248.72, compared with $6,604,650.50, an increase of $311,598.22.

Notes Payable 

As of June 30, 1953, notes payable amounted to $37,000, compared with $302,000 as of June 30, 1952.

The total endowment fund income for the year ended June 30, 1953, was $487,088.70, compared with $462,309.61 for the previous year, an increase of $24,779.09.

Rate Earned on Endowment Fund Assets 

The rate earned on the book value of total securities for the year ended June 30, 1953, was 4.37%, on the real estate 4.69%, and on the combined 4.41%. 

Gifts and Bequests 

The total amount received from gifts and bequests was $1,085,748.85 during the year ended June 30, 1953, compared with $467,750.28 during the previous year. 

Securities Portfolio 

As of June 30, 1953, the market value of securities held exceeded the book value by approximately $956,000.

During the year extensive purchases of long term bonds yielding between 3% and 4% were made with funds realized from the sale of short term issues. 

Inasmuch as most of these changes were made during the last quarter of the year, income was affected but slightly. A material improvement in the yield on the bond holdings should be realized in the current fiscal year, however. 

Respectfully submitted,

Nathan D. Bugbee 

Treasurer

Boston, November 23, 1953

To the Trustees of Tufts College, Tufts College, Massachusetts. 

We have examined the financial statements of Tufts College, as listed on the following page. Our examination was made in accordance with generally accepted auditing standards, and accordingly included such tests of the accounting records and such other auditing procedures as we considered necessary in the circumstances. 

In our opinion, subject to the qualification that the investments are stated at book values, the accompanying balance sheet and statements of income and expenditure and surplus present fairly the financial position of Tufts College at June 30, 1953, and the results of its operations for the year then ended, in conformity with generally accepted accounting principles for college and university accounting applied on a basis consistent with that of the preceding year. 

Scovell, Wellington & Company 5

TRUSTEES OF TUFTS COLLEGE BALLOU HALL HARVEY E. AVERILL ASSISTANT TREASURER SECRETARY MEDFORD 55, MASSACHUSETTS October 6, 1954 To Trustees of Tufts College Gentlemen:

Management Consultants MCDONALD, CURRIE & Ca CANADA COOPER BROTHERS & CO GREAT BRITAIN, EUROPE SOUTH AND EAST AFRICA SOUTHERN RHODESIA AUSTRALIA, NEW ZEALAND 

Boston, September 21, 1954

To the Trustees of Tufts College, 

Tufts College, Massachusetts.

We have examined the financial statements of Tufts College, as listed on the following page. Our examination was made in accordance with generally accepted auditing standards, and accordingly included such tests of the accounting records and such other auditing procedures as we considered necessary in the circumstances. 

In our opinion, subject to the qualification that the investments are stated at book values, the accompanying balance sheet and statement of income and expenditure and surplus present fairly the financial position of Tufts College at June 30, 1954, and the results of its operations for the year then ended, in conformity with generally accepted accounting principles for college and university accounting applied on a basis consistent with that of the preceding year. fuels, l'elling on Kasay 5 10
